Cognitive interventions focus on the negative or irrational thought patterns that lead to unhelpful or maladaptive behaviors. Henry Ford was correct when he stated, “Whether you think you can or think you cannot—you are right.” A child who believes that they will not do well on a test may feel helpless and not attempt to study a great deal, resulting in poor testing outcomes. That maladaptive scenario began with a negative thought about ability or potential outcomes of the test. In the same way, children with selective mutism may demonstrate negative thought patterns about speaking, socializing, or interacting verbally in the classroom or public settings. Some negative or maladaptive thoughts in children with selective mutism might include:
• “I just don’t have anything to say.”
• “I will not be able to say it correctly.”
• “If I talk, everyone will make a big deal.”
• “If I get the wrong answer, people will laugh at me.”
• “I have tried to talk before, and wasn’t able to get the words out then. What’s different this time?”
减少负面的想法
认知干预集中于导致无益或适应不良行为的消极或非理性思维模式。当亨利·福特说：“你是否认为自己能做到或者认为自己不能做到这一点时，他是正确的。”一个相信自己在考试中表现不佳的孩子可能会感到无助而不会尝试学习很多东西。在测试结果不佳。这种适应不良的情景始于对测试能力或潜在结果的负面想法。同样，有选择性缄默症的儿童可能会在课堂或公共环境中表现出口头，社交或口头交流的负面思维模式。选择性缄默症儿童的一些消极或适应不良的想法可能包括：
•“我只是无话可说。”
•“我无法正确说出来。”
•“如果我说话，每个人都会做出很大的贡献。”
•“如果我得到了错误的答案，人们会嘲笑我。”
•“我之前曾尝试过谈话，但当时无法说出来。这次有什么不同？“

Many of these self-statements have a kernel of truth, and therefore attempting to argue with the child may not be beneficial. Instead, it is beneficial to teach the child new self-statements, script and role-play for new events, and provide them with coping strategies in case some of their fears do come true.
First, we must identify the assumptions and perceptions that accompany the fear of speaking. If the child is unable to take this step, then cognitive interventions are not recommended. In order to identify the negative assumptions, adults could ask the child questions such as, “What makes it hard to talk?” or, “What are you thinking or saying to yourself when it is hard to talk?” Many times, children describe their experience to their parents, and therefore parents can be excellent reporters on what sorts of thoughts get in the way of speaking.
这些自我陈述中的许多都有真实的核心，因此试图与孩子争论可能没有益处。相反，教导孩子新事件的新自我陈述，剧本和角色扮演是有益的，并为他们提供应对策略，以防他们的某些恐惧成真。
首先，我们必须确定伴随着害怕说话的假设和观念。如果孩子无法采取这一步骤，则不建议进行认知干预。为了识别消极的假设，成年人可以问孩子这样的问题，例如“什么使得说话变得困难？”或者“当你难以说话时，你在想什么或者说什么呢？”很多时候，孩子们都在描述他们对父母的经历，因此父母可以成为优秀的记者，他们会说出什么样的想法。

If the child notes a fear of speaking incorrectly or giving an incorrect response, adults can modify their questions to reduce the fear of an incorrect answer. For example, teachers may ask the child questions in a forced-choice format, with only one obvious answer (the other choice being silly or obviously incorrect). Alternatively, the teachers may help the child practice responding in the classroom via shaping:
• First, the child practices raising their hand to show knowledge, with the understanding that the teacher will not call on them.
• Second, the teacher lets the child know that they will be calling on them during a specific time of the lesson, and tells the child exactly what they will be asking so that the child knows the answer with certainty.
• Third, the teacher calls on the child without preparation, but only if the child’s hand is raised. Children with SM should not be called on randomly if they are not indicating knowledge of the answer.
If the child fears that other children will overreact when they speak, the child could write a letter to peers asking them to please not “make a big deal out of it” when they do speak. It is important that peers and adults know to be matter-of-fact and calm when the child talks— pointing out that they spoke and asking them repeatedly to speak again likely increases the child’s anxiety.
Cognitive restructuring, or changing maladaptive thought patterns, involves identifying negative thoughts, challenging them, and replacing them with more appropriate, balanced, helpful thoughts. For example, if a child believes that because they were not able to speak in the past they will not be able to speak in the future, the interventionist can work with the child to determine whether that sort of statement holds true. Excellent workbooks exist for cognitive intervention, including Coping Cat by Phillip C. Kendall and Kristina A. Hedtke (www.copingcat.net) and Think Good, Feel Good by Paul Stallard for younger children and The Shyness and Social Anxiety Workbook for Teens by Jennifer Shannon for adolescents.
如果孩子注意到对说错误或给出错误回答的恐惧，成年人可以修改他们的问题以减少对错误答案的恐惧。例如，教师可能会以强制选择的形式询问孩子的问题，只有一个明显的答案（另一个选择是愚蠢或明显不正确）。或者，教师可以通过塑造以下方式帮助孩子在课堂上练习：
•首先，孩子练习举手表达知识，并明白老师不会拜访他们。
•其次，教师让孩子知道他们将在课程的特定时间内打电话给他们，并告诉孩子他们将要问的确切内容，以便孩子确切地知道答案。
•第三，老师在没有准备的情况下给孩子打电话，但只有孩子的手被抬起。如果没有表明对答案的了解，则不应随意呼叫患有SM的儿童。
如果孩子担心其他孩子在说话时会反应过度，孩子可以给同龄人写一封信，要求他们在说话时不要“大肆宣传”。重要的是，当孩子说话时，同龄人和成年人都知道事实和平静 - 指出他们说话并反复要求他们再说话可能会增加孩子的焦虑。
认知重组或改变适应不良的思维模式，包括识别消极思想，挑战它们，并用更恰当，平衡，有用的思想取而代之。例如，如果孩子认为因为他们过去不能说话而他们将来不会说话，那么干预主义者可以与孩子一起确定这种陈述是否成立。优秀的工作手册可用于认知干预，包括Phillip C. Kendall和Kristina A. Hedtke（www.copingcat.net）的Coping Cat和Paul Stallard为年幼孩子设计的Think Good，Feel Good以及Jennifer的青少年害羞和社交焦虑工作手册香农为青少年。

INCREASING SELF-CONFIDENCE
Think of a time recently when you felt really self-confident. What were you doing? What had just occurred? For most of us, self-confidence comes both from feeling that others value you and feeling successful, worthy, or talented. Some ways to increase self-confidence in kids with selective mutism include:
• Spend uninterrupted time with your child. Play what they want to play. Talk about what they want to talk about. Do what they want to do, and follow their lead. Special time like this helps children feel valued, loved, and appreciated.
• Help the child build success in speaking through the slow, steady steps outlined in this book.
Success builds on success, and even small steps are movements in the right direction.
• Make a “Brave Chart” for your refrigerator at home (if you are a parent) or office at school
(if you are a keyworker), and list the brave activities or brave work your child has accomplished. Remind them frequently of all their accomplishments, and how much braver and stronger they have become. Encourage teens to document on their phones or journal all of the steps they have taken (even the small ones) and look back at it on a regular basis.
• Praise success, but also praise efforts. Life is not always about outcomes; sometimes children try
very hard but are not quite successful. They should be praised for their efforts.
• Encourage the child to develop talents that are not speech-based. For example, many children
with SM are wonderful singers, beautiful dancers, or talented soccer players. Children need to feel that they are good at something, and these extracurricular activities not only allow them to feel talented but also provide excellent social opportunities for brave practices.
• Display self-confidence yourself. Self-confidence is not the absence of worry or nervousness; it is the ability to stand and act in the face of those feelings. When a situation comes up that makes you feel nervous, tell the child your worries (assuming that the situation is age-appropriate and does not directly involve the child). Then, tell the child the confident self-statements that help you be brave in the face of worry. For example, if you have a big deadline at work, you might explain to the child that you feel nervous thinking that you cannot finish in time. However, you have finished projects before deadlines in the past, and you know that if you work hard you are completely capable of finishing.

提高自信心
想想最近你真正自信的时候。你在做什么？刚刚发生了什么？对于我们大多数人来说，自信来自感觉别人重视你并感受到成功，有价值或有才能。一些提高选择性缄默症儿童自信心的方法包括：
•与孩子一起度过不间断的时间。玩他们想玩的东西。谈谈他们想谈什么。做他们想做的事，并跟随他们的领导。这样的特殊时间可以帮助孩子感受到被重视，被爱和被欣赏。
•通过本书中概述的缓慢，稳定的步骤，帮助孩子成功说话。
成功建立在成功的基础上，即使是小步骤也是朝着正确方向发展的。
•为您家中的冰箱（如果您是父母）或学校办公室制作“勇敢的图表”
（如果您是一名关键工作者），并列出您的孩子已经完成的勇敢的活动或勇敢的工作。经常提醒他们所有的成就，以及他们变得多么勇敢和坚强。鼓励青少年在他们的手机或日志上记录他们采取的所有步骤（即使是小步骤），并定期回顾它。
•赞美成功，也赞美努力。生活并不总是关于结果;有时孩子会尝试
非常努力但不太成功。应该赞扬他们的努力。
•鼓励孩子培养成为不依赖言语能力的人才。例如，很多孩子与SM是美妙的歌手，美丽的舞者，或有才华的足球运动员。孩子们需要感觉自己擅长某些事情，而这些课外活动不仅让他们感到有才能，而且还为勇敢的实践提供了极好的社交机会。
•自己展示自信。自信不是没有忧虑或紧张;它是站在这些感情面前表现和行动的能力。当出现让您感到紧张的情况时，请告诉孩子您的担忧（假设情况适合年龄并且不直接涉及孩子）。然后，告诉孩子自信的自我陈述，帮助你在面对忧虑时勇敢。例如，如果您的工作期限很长，您可以向孩子解释您认为自己无法及时完成的​​紧张感。但是，您在过去的截止日期之前完成了项目，并且您知道如果您努力工作，您完全有能力完成。

SOCIAL SKILLS TRAINING 
Research does not demonstrate a fundamental deficit in social skill acquisition for children with selective mutism. However, since kids with SM have significantly fewer social interactions, it is not uncommon that they are socially immature, lack some social awareness, or don’t demonstrate age-appropriate social skills. Social skills training or role-playing social interactions can be helpful for two reasons.
First, it helps children “script” social situations. A script is a sequence of expected behaviors for a situation. Most adults have a script for social situations. If you are going to the doctor’s office, the script likely includes checking in, sitting in the waiting room until you are called, relocating to an examination room, and speaking to the physician. Then you will check out at the front desk and leave. Without a script, anxiety and uncertainty would reign. What if you were going to go to the doctor’s office, but you had no idea what would occur there? You did not know how much you were expected to pay, what the doctor would do or say, whether you would be safe or unsafe, and who might be allowed to see you in one of those open-backed gowns. In situations without scripts, most individuals feel ill-prepared, uncertain, anxious, and avoidant. Scripts help not only to reduce anxiety but also to build new language, social, and activity routines for children (Barnett et al., 2007). Possible scripts include how to order at a restaurant, interrupt someone who is not already attentive to you, introduce yourself, ask a friend to play, etc.
社交技能培训
研究并未证明选择性缄默症儿童的社会技能获取存在根本性缺陷。然而，由于患有SM的孩子的社交互动明显较少，因此他们在社交方面不成熟，缺乏一些社会意识或不展示适合年龄的社交技能的情况并不少见。社交技能培训或角色扮演社交互动可能有两个原因。
首先，它有助于儿童“编写”社交场合。脚本是一种情境的预期行为序列。大多数成年人都有社交场合的剧本。如果你去医生办公室，脚本可能包括办理入住手续，坐在候诊室，直到你被叫，搬到检查室，并与医生交谈。然后，您将在前台退房并离开。如果没有剧本，焦虑和不确定将会统治。如果你要去医生办公室怎么办，但你不知道那里会发生什么？您不知道您应该支付多少钱，医生会做什么或说什么，您是安全还是不安全，以及谁可能被允许在其中一件开背式礼服中看到您。在没有脚本的情况下，大多数人都感到准备不足，不确定，焦虑和回避。脚本不仅有助于减少焦虑，还有助于为儿童建立新的语言，社交和活动习惯（Barnett等，2007）。可能的脚本包括如何在餐厅订购，打断一个尚未关注您的人，介绍自己，请朋友玩，等等。

Second, social skills training allows the opportunity to learn and practice social interactions in a safe, comfortable setting. When teaching a new skill, adults often stop when the child seems to have initially mastered the skill, and then expect that the child will easily utilize that new skill in each and every situation. “Overlearning” is a term used for practicing much beyond the initial mastery of a newly acquired skill; overlearning leads to expanded mastery and automaticity of a skill. The Yerkes-Dodson law predicts that overlearning will significantly increase the use of the behavior even in high-arousal states (Rohrer, 2005). In layman’s terms, if a child practices a social behavior to the point of complete and total mastery and even beyond to the point of boredom, it becomes automatic and therefore can be done in their sleep, with their eyes closed, and standing on their head. This is exactly what we want for children with SM when they approach the counter at McDonalds® and the clerk asks them what they would like, their response and the back-and-forth banter of ordering should be so ingrained and over- practiced that, despite their increasing level of anxiety (and therefore emotional arousal), they are simply programmed to respond without even thinking. This type of over-practice takes. . . a lot of practice. Role playing should be part of the child’s normal day. Children should be role-playing with parents, keyworkers, therapists, and others in preparation for social interactions. A good rule of thumb is that the more realistic the scenario, the more effective the role-play. For example, if the role-lay is in preparation for an upcoming presentation, it is wonderful to practice that presentation at home, in the car, and at a friend’s house. However, the most effective role-play might be in the classroom where the presentation will be given, at the microphone, and with the prompts that will be used in the actual presentation. Likewise, if a child is role-playing to ask a peer over for a play date, a role-play done on the playground (where they are planning to ask) and with another peer whom they already feel comfortable with is likely to be most effective.
其次，社交技能培训可以让您有机会在安全，舒适的环境中学习和练习社交互动。当教授新技能时，成人经常在孩子最初掌握技能时停止，然后期望孩子在每种情况下都能轻松利用这项新技能。 “过度学习”这个术语用于练习远远超出最新掌握的技能;过度学习可以提高技能的掌握和自动化程度。 Yerkes-Dodson法则预测即使在高觉醒状态下，过度学习也会显着增加行为的使用（Rohrer，2005）。用外行人的话来说，如果一个孩子将社会行为发挥到完全和完全掌握的程度，甚至超出无聊的程度，它就会变得自动化，因此可以在他们的睡眠中完成，闭着眼睛，站在他们的头上。这正是我们想要SM的孩子们在McDonalds®接近柜台时所需要的，并且店员问他们想要什么，他们的反应以及来回的嘲讽应该是如此根深蒂固和过度练习，尽管他们的焦虑程度越来越高（因此情绪激动），但他们只是在没有思考的情况下做出反应。这种过度练习需要。 。 。很多练习。角色扮演应该是孩子正常日子的一部分。儿童应该与父母，关键工作者，治疗师和其他人一起进行角色扮演，为社交互动做准备。一个好的经验法则是场景越真实，角色扮演越有效。例如，如果角色扮演正在为即将到来的演示做准备，那么在家里，车内和朋友家里练习演示是很棒的。但是，最有效的角色扮演可能是在演示文稿的教室，麦克风以及将在实际演示中使用的提示。同样地，如果一个孩子在角色扮演中向同伴询问一个游戏日期，那么在操场上（他们计划要求的地方）和他们已经感觉舒适的另一个同伴进行的角色扮演可能是最多的。有效。

Social stories also offer social skills training through a medium most children find entertaining— books, videos, and phone apps. A social story is a visual or written guide to a variety of social interactions, situations, or skills for which the child may need preparation. The goal of social stories is to provide accurate and understandable steps in a social situation, allowing the child to see the likely stages and outcomes of an experience prior to experiencing it themselves. There are many sites providing general social stories, such as Sandbox Learning (http://www.sandbox-learning.com/) or the Social Story® Creator and Library® app by Touch Autism. It is also easy and fun to create your own social stories by making a book with your child relating how a social interaction will take place.
社交故事还提供社交技能培训，通过大多数孩子找到娱乐的媒体 - 书籍，视频和电话应用程序。社交故事是对儿童可能需要准备的各种社交互动，情境或技能的视觉或书面指南。社交故事的目标是在社交场合提供准确且易于理解的步骤，让孩子在体验之前了解体验的可能阶段和结果。有许多网站提供一般社交故事，例如Sandbox Learning（http://www.sandbox-learning.com/）或Touch Autism的SocialMonsus®Creator和Library®应用程序。通过与孩子一起制作关于如何进行社交互动的书来创建自己的社交故事也很容易和有趣。

PREDICTABILITY AND CONTROL
Humans feel anxiety because of a lack of predictability and a feeling of a lack of control. If something is under your control and you know exactly what will happen, you are not worried or stressed about it. We only worry when we do not feel in control or do not know what will happen next.
Children with selective mutism also desire predictability and control. Certain things cannot be completely controlled, such as the social responses children might receive from peers when they speak (although we can try to remind peers of the appropriate response when speech occurs). However, the more control and predictability we can provide to a child with selective mutism, the more the child will feel a reduction in anxiety and a feeling of mastery. For example:
Be consistent. I cannot highlight enough the importance of being consistent with anxious children. Consistency takes many forms:
可预测性和控制
由于缺乏可预测性和缺乏控制感，人类感到焦虑。如果某些东西在您的控制之下并且您确切知道将会发生什么，那么您并不担心或不会对此感到担忧。当我们感觉不到控制或者不知道接下来会发生什么时，我们才会担心。
选择性缄默症的儿童也需要可预测性和控制力。某些事情无法完全控制，例如孩子们说话时孩子们可能从同龄人那里得到的社会反应（尽管我们可以尝试在发言时提醒同伴适当的反应）。然而，我们可以为有选择性缄默症的孩子提供更多的控制和可预测性，孩子会越多地感到焦虑减少和掌握感。例如：
始终如一。我无法强调与焦虑的孩子保持一致的重要性。一致性有多种形式：

• Be consistent and predictable in your intervention. Kids should understand the next steps, the overall goal of getting braver, and the strategies that will be used. My sessions always run in the exact same manner—I am very predictable. The activity might be different, and the goal changes from session to session, but the child knows how long the meeting will last, what sorts of steps we will be working on, and that I will never move too quickly for them.
• Be consistent with your discipline and expectations. I strongly believe that children with selective mutism should not be given a “pass” on unacceptable behavior. Many teachers tell me that they allow the child with SM to get away with not following the rules of the classroom because they don’t want to upset him or her. For example, a teacher recently told me that a patient of mine had been blatantly copying off of another student in the classroom, right under the eyes of the teacher. She knew it was going on, and had reminded the class as a whole of the rules, but he had not stopped and she had done nothing about it because she was afraid of his reaction. Of course we must first make sure that there is nothing else prohibiting the child from doing his own work (i.e., Does he have a learning disorder and not understand? Was there a lack of understanding of the directions, but he could not ask for clarification so he was leaning over to determine what he should be doing?). We considered all of these possibilities and came to the conclusion that he was simply cheating. I gave permission to the teacher to tell him in no uncertain terms that cheating would no longer be accepted, and he immediately stopped. Children with SM must be held to the same behavioral expectations as peers, assuming that the behavior is not speech-related.
•在干预中保持一致和可预测。孩子们应该了解接下来的步骤，勇敢的总体目标以及将要使用的策略。我的会话总是以完全相同的方式运行 - 我非常容易预测。活动可能会有所不同，目标会话会话也会发生变化，但是孩子知道会议将持续多长时间，我们将采取哪些步骤，以及我永远不会为他们提速过快。
•与您的纪律和期望保持一致。我坚信，选择性缄默症的孩子不应该对不可接受的行为给予“通过”。许多老师告诉我，他们允许有SM的孩子逃脱不遵守课堂规则，因为他们不想让他或她不高兴。例如，一位老师最近告诉我，我的一名病人在教室的眼睛下公然抄袭了另一名学生。她知道它正在发生，并提醒整个班级的规则，但他没有停止，她没有做任何事情，因为她害怕他的反应。当然，我们必须首先确保没有别的东西禁止孩子做自己的工作（即，他是否有学习障碍而不理解？是否缺乏对方向的理解，但他无法要求澄清所以他倾向于确定他应该做什么？）。我们考虑了所有这些可能性，并得出结论他只是在作弊。我允许老师毫不含糊地告诉他，作弊将不再被接受，他立即停止了。 SM的儿童必须与同龄人保持相同的行为期望，假设行为与言语无关。

• Be consistent in the classroom. Children with SM tend to enjoy visual schedules. If there is going to be a significant change in the day, let them know the change and how that will impact them. If possible, keep their school day consistent and predictable.
•在课堂上保持一致。患有SM的儿童倾向于享受视觉时间表。如果当天会发生重大变化，请让他们知道变化以及影响他们的方式。如果可能的话，保持他们的学校日一致和可预测。

• Be consistent at home. Kids need a predictable daily schedule, predictable bedtimes, predictable routines, and predictable responses from parents. Children with anxiety thrive on predictability and consistency in the home. I know how difficult it can be to stay predictable—life throws curveballs in terms of scheduling, parents are tired and are not always consistent with discipline approaches, and activities sometimes allow kids to stay up much later than they should. I understand that life can be chaotic, but be as predictable as possible.
•在家里保持一致。孩子们需要一个可预测的日常安排，可预测的就寝时​​间，可预测的例行程序以及来自父母的可预测的回答。患有焦虑症的儿童在家中的可预测性和一致性上茁壮成长。我知道在日程安排方面保持可预测的生活是多么困难，父母感到厌倦并且并不总是与纪律方法保持一致，而且活动有时会让孩子们比他们应该更晚地熬夜。我知道生活可能是混乱的，但尽可能可预测。

• Give the child some control over their intervention. We learn as parents that kids need to have a sense of control in their daily lives, such as picking out their own clothing and deciding what they want to drink with dinner. We quickly learn to give them choices that we as adults are equally comfortable with; in that way, both the child and adult can be happy with the outcome. The same strategy can be used in behavioral intervention. For example, when I move to the step of blowing air, I often give children several choices of how we can practice blowing air (i.e., “We are going to practice blowing air, and we could use a balloon, a pinwheel, or a candle— which would you like to use to practice?”). I am equally comfortable with all of those options, but I am giving the child the ability to decide what type of practice we will do. At the step of making sounds, I may put letters of the alphabet on cards and allow the child to choose which sounds to start with. Eventually we will do all of the sounds, but it gives the child a sense of control over the progression. When it is time to speak to someone new or use their voice in a new setting, I give many choices of people or places and allow the child to pick. I do not ask the child if they would like to blow air, or if they would like to talk to someone new, because I can almost guarantee what the answer will be. Instead, I give several acceptable options. 
•让孩子控制他们的干预。我们作为父母学习，孩子们需要在日常生活中有一种控制感，比如挑选自己的衣服，决定他们想要在晚餐时喝什么。我们很快就会学会给予他们成年人同样舒适的选择;通过这种方式，孩子和成人都可以对结果感到满意。相同的策略可用于行为干预。例如，当我转向吹气的步骤时，我经常给孩子们选择如何练习吹空气（即“我们要练习吹空气，我们可以使用气球，风车或者蜡烛 - 你想用它练习吗？“）。我对所有这些选择同样感到满意，但我让孩子有能力决定我们将要做什么类型的练习。在发出声音的步骤中，我可以在卡片上放置字母，让孩子选择开始的声音。最终我们会做所有的声音，但它会让孩子对进展有一种控制感。当是时候与新人交谈或在新环境中使用他们的声音时，我会给出很多人或地方的选择并让孩子选择。我不会问孩子他们是否想要吹气，或者他们是否愿意与新人交谈，因为我几乎可以保证答案是什么。相反，我给出了几个可接受的选项。

THE ART OF RELAXING
Relaxation training is a common behavioral intervention for anxiety, and training children with selective mutism how to relax their bodies and minds is valuable. Typically, teaching relaxation training at the start of the intervention proves to be difficult, since relaxation training generally includes deep breathing (which may be anxiety-provoking when children are beginning treatment with a novel adult). Thus, I either train parents on how to teach their child to relax at home or I wait until the child begins to feel comfortable with me in the clinic before I begin relaxation training.
Common characteristics of children with SM include, 1) becoming easily aroused in social or public situations; 2) stiff, awkward body postures due to anxiety; and, 3) difficulty returning to baseline. Progressive muscle relaxation, along with mindfulness training (i.e., training in the awareness of thoughts, feelings, and physiological responses) and diaphragmatic breathing helps teach body awareness and awareness of breathing and the senses. Often, children with SM are extremely guarded with their body movements and are too embarrassed to participate in relaxation strategies with a therapist. Working first on mindfulness, including mindful listening, mindful observing, and mindful touching can be less anxiety-provoking and therefore a good place to start. The MindUP Curriculum by the Hawn Foundation outlines activities that can be used to teach mindfulness to any age group.
When a child becomes more comfortable in the clinic, progressive muscle relaxation and diaphragmatic breathing can be taught through a variety of methods. Scripts are available for progressive muscle relaxation; these scripts walk children verbally through the tensing and relaxing of their major muscle groups. I Can Relax! by Donna Pincus and Still Quiet Place: Mindfulness for Young Children by Amy Salzman provides sound effects and music with a narrator who leads the child through muscle relaxation and visualization.
放松的艺术
放松训练是对焦虑的常见行为干预，并且训练有选择性缄默症的儿童如何放松自己的身体和心灵是有价值的。通常，在干预开始时教授放松训练证明是困难的，因为放松训练通常包括深呼吸（当儿童开始用新成人治疗时可能引起焦虑）。因此，我或者培训父母如何教他们的孩子在家里放松，或者等到孩子在我开始放松训练之前开始对我感到舒服。
SM儿童的共同特征包括：1）在社交或公共场合容易引起; 2）因焦虑而僵硬，笨拙的身体姿势; 3）难以返回基线。渐进式肌肉放松，以及正念训练（即，对思想，感觉和生理反应的意识训练）和膈肌呼吸有助于教导身体意识和呼吸和感官意识。通常情况下，SM患儿的身体动作受到极大的保护，并且过于尴尬，无法与治疗师一起参与放松策略。首先注意正念，包括有意识的倾听，有意识的观察和有意识的触摸可以减少引起焦虑，因此是一个开始的好地方。霍恩基金会的MindUP课程概述了可用于教授任何年龄组的正念的活动。
当孩子在诊所变得更加舒适时，可以通过各种方法教授渐进性肌肉松弛和膈肌呼吸。脚本可用于渐进式肌肉放松;这些剧本通过对主要肌肉群的紧张和放松来口头表达儿童。我可以放松！作者：Donna Pincus和静谧的地方：Amy Salzman为幼儿正念提供声音效果和音乐，并通过肌肉放松和可视化引导孩子的叙述者。

An example of a progressive muscle relaxation script might include (Adapted from Koeppen, 1974):
PROGRESSIVE MUSCLE RELAXATION SCRIPT “Today we’re going to practice some special kinds of exercises called relaxation exercises. These exercises help you to learn how to relax when you’re feeling uptight and help you get rid of those butterflies-in-your-stomach kinds of feelings. They’re also kind of neat because you can learn how to do some of them without anyone really noticing.
In order for you to get the best feelings from these exercises, there are some rules you must follow. First, you need to do exactly what I say, even if it seems kind of silly. Second, you must try hard to do what I say. Third, you must pay attention to your body. Throughout these exercises, pay attention to how your muscles feel when they are tight and when they are loose and relaxed. And fourth, you must practice. The more you practice, the more relaxed you can get. Do you have any questions?
Are you ready to begin? Okay, first, get as comfortable as you can in your chair. Sit back, get both feet on the floor, and just let your arms hang loose. That’s fine. Now close your eyes and try to keep them closed as we practice. Remember to follow my instructions very carefully, try hard, and pay attention to your body. Here we go.”

渐进式肌肉放松脚本的一个例子可能包括（改编自Koeppen，1974）：

渐进式肌肉放松脚本“今天我们要练习一些叫做放松练习的特殊练习。这些练习可以帮助你学习如何放松，当你感到紧张，并帮助你摆脱那些蝴蝶在你胃里的感觉。他们也很整洁，因为你可以学习如何做一些没有人真正注意到的。
为了让您从这些练习中获得最佳感受，您必须遵循一些规则。首先，你需要完全按照我说的做，即使它看起来有点傻。其次，你必须努力做我说的话。第三，你必须注意你的身体。在这些练习中，要注意肌肉在紧身时以及松弛和放松时的肌肉感受。第四，你必须练习。练习越多，你就越放松。你有任何问题吗？
你准备好开始吗？好的，首先，尽量让你坐在椅子上。坐下来，双脚放在地板上，然后让你的手臂松开。没关系。现在闭上眼睛，试着在练习时让它们闭合。记得要非常小心地遵循我的指示，努力，并注意你的身体。开始了。”

Pretend you have a whole lemon in your left hand. Now squeeze it hard. Try to squeeze all the juice out. Feel the tightness in your hand and arm as you squeeze. Now drop the lemon. Notice how your muscles feel when they are relaxed. Take another lemon and squeeze. Try to squeeze this one harder than you did the first one. That’s right. Real hard. Now drop the lemon and relax. See how much better your hand and arm feel when they are relaxed. Once again, take a lemon in your left hand and squeeze all the juice out. Don’t leave a single drop. Squeeze hard. Good. Now relax and let the lemon fall from your hand. (Repeat the process for the right hand and arm.)
假装左手拿着一整个柠檬。现在用力挤压它。尽量挤出所有的果汁。当你挤压时，感觉手和手臂的紧绷感。现在放下柠檬。注意放松时肌肉的感觉。拿另一个柠檬挤压。尝试比第一个更难挤压这个。那就对了。真的很难。现在放下柠檬放松一下。看看放松时手和手臂的感觉有多好。再次，用左手拿柠檬，挤出所有的果汁。不要留下一滴。挤得很厉害。好。现在放松，让柠檬从你手中掉下来。 （重复右手和手臂的过程。）

Pretend you are a furry, lazy cat. You want to stretch. Stretch your arms out in front of you. Raise them up high over your head. Way back. Feel the pull in your shoulders. Stretch higher. Now just let your arms drop back to your side. Okay, let’s stretch again. Stretch your arms out in front of you. Raise them over your head. Pull them back, way back. Pull hard. Now let them drop quickly. Good. Notice how your shoulders feel more relaxed. This time let’s have a great big stretch. Try to touch the ceiling. Stretch your arms way out in front of you. Raise them way up high over your head. Push them way, way back. Notice the tension and pull in your arms and shoulders. Hold tight, now. Great. Let them drop very quickly and feel how good it is to be relaxed. It feels good and warm and lazy.
假装你是一只毛茸茸的懒猫。你想伸展。伸展双臂在你面前。将它们抬高到头顶。回来了。感受肩膀的拉力。伸展得更高。现在让你的手臂回到你身边。好的，我们再来一次。伸展双臂在你面前。抬起头来。向后拉回来。拉硬了。现在让他们迅速下降。好。注意你的肩膀感觉更放松。这一次让我们有一个很大的延伸。试着触摸天花板。在你面前伸展双臂。把它们提升到你的头顶。回过头来推它们。注意张力并拉动你的手臂和肩膀。紧紧抓住，现在。大。让他们快速下降，感受放松是多么美好。它感觉良好，温暖和懒惰。

Now pretend you have a giant jawbreaker bubble gum in your mouth. It’s very hard to chew. Bite down on it. Hard. Let your neck muscles help you. Now relax. Just let your jaw hang loose. Notice that how good it feels just to let your jaw drop. Okay, let’s tackle that jawbreaker again now. Bite down. Hard. Try to squeeze it out between your teeth. That’s good. You’re really tearing that gum up. Now relax again. It feels good just to let go and not have to fight that bubble gum. Okay, one more time. Bite down. Hard as you can. Oh, you’re really working hard. Good. Now relax. Try to relax your whole body. Let yourself go as loose as you can.
现在假装你口中有一个巨大的破口泡泡糖。这很难咀嚼。咬下它。硬。让你的颈部肌肉帮助你。现在放松一下只是让你的下巴松动。注意让你的下巴掉下来感觉有多好。好的，让我们现在再次解决这个令人伤心的问题。咬下来。硬。试着在牙齿之间挤出来。那很好。你真的撕裂了那个口香糖。现在再放松一下。只是放手而不必打泡泡糖感觉很好。好的，还有一次。咬下来。尽你所能。哦，你真的很努力。好。现在放松一下尽量放松你的全身。让自己尽可能地松散。

Here comes a pesky old fly. He has landed on your nose. Try to get him off without using your hands. That’s right, wrinkle up your nose. Make as many wrinkles in your nose as you can. Scrunch your nose up real hard. Good. You’ve chased him away. Now you can relax your nose. Oops, here he comes back again. Right back in the middle of your nose. Wrinkle up your nose again. Shoo him off. Wrinkle it up hard. Hold it just as tight as you can. Okay, he flew away. You can relax your face. Notice that when you scrunch up your nose, your cheeks and your mouth and your forehead and your eyes all help you, and they get tight too. So when you relax your nose, your whole body relaxes too, and that feels good. Oh-oh. This time that old fly has come back, but this time he’s on your forehead. Make lots of wrinkles. Try to catch him between all those wrinkles. Hold it tight, now. Okay, you can let go. He’s gone for good. Now you can just relax. Let your face go smooth, no wrinkles anywhere. Your face feels nice and smooth and relaxed.
这是一个讨厌的老苍蝇。他落在了你的鼻子上。试着不用你的双手让他离开。没错，你的鼻子皱了起来。尽可能多地在鼻子上留下皱纹。真的很努力地抬起你的鼻子。好。你把他赶走了。现在你可以放松你的鼻子。哎呀，他又来了。回到你的鼻子中间。再次皱起鼻子。把他赶走皱得很厉害。尽量紧紧抓住它。好的，他飞走了。你可以放松你的脸。请注意，当你揉鼻子时，你的脸颊和嘴巴，前额和眼睛都会帮助你，而且它们也会紧张。因此，当你放松鼻子时，你的整个身体也会放松，感觉很好。哦，哦。这一次老苍蝇回来了，但这次他在你的额头上。皱纹很多。试着在所有皱纹之间抓住他。现在紧紧抓住它。好的，你可以放手。他已经走了。现在你可以放松一下。让你的脸变得光滑，任何地方都没有皱纹。你的脸感觉美丽，光滑，放松。
Hey. Here comes a cute baby elephant. But he’s not watching where he’s going. He doesn’t see you lying in the grass, and he’s about to step on your stomach. Don’t move. You don’t have time to get out of the way. Just get ready for him. Make your stomach very hard. Tighten up your stomach muscles real tight. Hold it. It looks like he is going the other way. You can relax now. Let your stomach go soft. Let it be as relaxed as you can. That feels so much better. Oops, he’s coming this way again. Get Ready. Tighten up your stomach. Make your stomach into a rock. Okay, he’s moving away again. You can relax now. Kind of settle down, get comfortable, and relax. Notice the difference between a tight stomach and a relaxed one. That’s how we want to feel—nice and loose and relaxed. You won’t believe this, but this time he’s coming your way and no turning around. He’s headed straight for you. Tighten up. Here he comes. This is really it. You’ve got to hold on tight. He’s stepping on you. He’s stepped over you. Now he’s gone for good. You can relax completely. You’re safe. Everything is okay, and you can feel nice and relaxed.
嘿。这是一个可爱的小象。但他并不在意他要去的地方。他没有看到你躺在草地上，他正要踩到你的肚子。别动。你没有时间摆脱困境。为他做好准备让你的胃非常坚硬。紧紧收紧你的腹部肌肉。拿住。看起来他正在走另一条路。你现在可以放松一下。让你的胃变软。让它尽可能放松。感觉好多了。哎呀，他又来了。做好准备。收紧你的胃。让你的胃成为一块石头。好的，他又搬走了。你现在可以放松一下。有点安定下来，舒服，放松。注意紧张的胃和放松的胃之间的区别。这就是我们想要的感觉 - 好看，宽松和放松。你不会相信这一点，但这一次他是你的方式，没有转身。他直奔你。勒紧，加强。他来了。这是真的。你必须紧紧抓住。他踩着你。他踩到了你。现在他已经走了。你可以完全放松。你安全了。一切都很好，你可以感觉良好和放松。

This time imagine that you want to squeeze through a narrow fence and the boards have splinters on them. You’ll have to make yourself very skinny if you’re going to make it through. Suck your stomach in. Try to squeeze it up against your backbone. Try to be skinny as you can. You’ve got to be skinny now. Just relax and feel your stomach being warm and loose. Okay, let’s try to get through that fence now. Squeeze up your stomach. Make it touch your backbone. Get it real small and tight. Get it as skinny as you can. Hold tight, now. You’ve got to squeeze through. You got through that narrow little fence and no splinters. You can relax now. Settle back and let your stomach come back out where it belongs. You can feel really good now. You’ve done fine.
这一次想象你想挤过一个狭窄的栅栏，板上有碎片。如果你要完成它，你必须让自己变得非常瘦。吮吸你的胃。尝试将它挤在你的骨干上。尽量做到瘦。你现在必须要瘦。放松一下，感觉肚子温暖而松散。好的，我们现在试着通过那个围栏。挤压你的胃。让它触及你的脊梁。让它真实小而紧。尽可能地让它变瘦。紧紧抓住，现在。你必须挤过去。你穿过狭窄的小篱笆，没有碎片。你现在可以放松一下。安顿回来，让你的胃回到它所属的地方。你现在感觉很好。你做得很好。

Now pretend that you are standing barefoot in a big, fat mud puddle. Squish your toes down deep into the mud. Try to get your feet down to the bottom of the mud puddle. You’ll probably need your legs to help you push. Push down, spread your toes apart, feel the mud squish up between your toes. Now step out of the mud puddle. Relax your feet. Let your toes go loose and feel how nice that it feels to be relaxed. Back into the mud puddle. Squish your toes down. Let your leg muscles help push your feet down. Push your feet. Hard. Try to squeeze that puddle dry. Okay. Come back out now. Relax your feet, relax your legs, relax your toes. It feels so good to be relaxed. No tenseness anywhere. You feel kind of warm and tingly.
现在假装你赤脚站在一个大的深的泥泞水坑里。把你的脚趾深深地塞进泥里。尽量让你的脚踏到泥坑的底部。你可能需要你的腿来帮助你推动。向下推，将脚趾分开，感觉脚趾间的泥浆挤压。现在走出泥坑。放松你的脚。让你的脚趾松弛，感觉放松的感觉有多好。回到泥泞的水坑里。压下你的脚趾。让你的腿部肌肉帮助你的脚向下推。推你的脚。硬。尽量挤干水坑。好的。现在回来吧。放松双脚，放松双腿，放松脚趾。放松感觉真好。任何地方都没有紧张感。你觉得有点温暖和刺激。

Stay as relaxed as you can. Let your whole body go limp and feel all your muscles relaxed. In a few minutes I will ask you to open your eyes, and that will be the end of this practice session. As you go through the day, remember how good it feels to be relaxed. Sometimes you have to make yourself tighter before you can be relaxed, just as we did in these exercises. Practice these exercises everyday to get more and more relaxed. A good time to practice is at night, after you have gone to bed and the lights are out and you won’t be disturbed. It will help you get to sleep. Then, when you are really a good relaxer, you can help yourself relax at school. Just remember the elephant, or the jaw breaker, or the mud puddle, and you can do our exercises and nobody will know. Today is a good day, and you are ready to feel very relaxed. You’ve worked hard and it feels good to work hard. Very slowly, now, open your eyes and wiggle your muscles around a little. Very good. You’ve done a good job. You’re going to be a super relaxer.
尽可能保持放松。让你的整个身体跛行，让你的肌肉放松。几分钟后，我会请你睁开眼睛，这将是本练习课程的结束。当你度过这一天时，请记住放松的感觉。有时你必须在放松之前让自己变得更紧，就像我们在这些练习中所做的那样。每天练习这些练习，以获得越来越多的放松。练习的好时机是晚上，在你上床睡觉后灯光熄灭你不会被打扰。它会帮助你入睡。然后，当你真的是一个很好的放松者，你可以帮助自己在学校放松。只要记住大象，或下巴破碎者，或泥水坑，你可以做我们的练习，没有人会知道。今天是美好的一天，你准备好感到非常放松。你努力工作，努力工作感觉很好。非常缓慢，现在，睁开眼睛，稍微摆动你的肌肉。很好。你做得很好。你将成为一个超级放松者。

Apps are also available for bodily self-awareness and relaxation, including Cardiograph (an app measuring heart rate through the iPhone) and BellyBio® (interactive breathing through biofeedback). Children tend to find these apps fun and engaging, and it keeps them interested in the relaxation process. Biodots® (www.biodots.net) are also enjoyable additions to relaxation training. Biodots are small stickers which gauge skin temperature (a common measurement of physiological stress and anxiety) and change colors accordingly. After teaching children how to relax, they are sent home with the project of practicing relaxation, since skills cannot be used in the midst of stress unless they are practiced frequently. Parents and children log their heart rate and Biodot® color before and after relaxation in order to observe change through relaxation.
应用程序还可用于身体自我意识和放松，包括心电图（一种通过iPhone测量心率的应用程序）和BellyBio®（通过生物反馈进行交互式呼吸）。孩子们倾向于发现这些应用程序既有趣又吸引人，并且让他们对放松过程感兴趣。 Biodots®（www.biodots.net）也是放松训练的有趣补充。 Biodots是小贴纸，用于测量皮肤温度（生理压力和焦虑的常见测量）并相应地改变颜色。在教孩子们如何放松之后，他们被送回家进行放松练习的项目，因为技能不能在压力中使用，除非他们经常练习。父母和孩子在放松前后记录他们的心率和Biodot®颜色，以便通过放松观察变化。

CONCLUSION
Physicians, psychologists, teachers, and others constantly sing the praises of early intervention for a multitude of developmental and psychological disorders in childhood. Early intervention of special needs can improve outcomes, lessen the effects of the condition, and teach others to support the child and family in a successful manner. Too frequently children with selective mutism do not receive early intervention. The most common reasons include:
• A lag in time between observation of an issue (i.e., the difficulty speaking in public) and diagnosis/intervention. If you are reading this book, you are better prepared to help curb this delay (congratulations). Please consider helping others find out about selective mutism by educating your school staff, pediatrician, family, and friends. Send out informational brochures. Talk to others about your experience. Spread awareness.
• Difficulty finding specialists who can help. This is a particularly difficult issue, because many psychologists still do not know enough about selective mutism. Look specifically for a psychologist with experience treating kids with anxiety using behavioral therapy, or visit www.selectivemutism.org, www.adaa.org, or www.abct.org to find appropriate referrals.
• Concerns about “labeling” the child. Trust me, the child knows that they do not talk. This is no secret and it is not a valid reason for continuing without intervention.
• Family members who are not supportive. Everyone has one—a family member who believes the child is “just fine” or thinks that all they need is more discipline in the home. One of the hardest parts about being a parent is making unpopular decisions with the knowledge that it will improve the child’s life in the long-run.
• Belief that the child will grow out of it. There are children with SM who improve significantly with time and maturity. And there are children who do not improve with time, and instead experience an increase in anxiety, a reduction in friends, and difficulty being successful in school. We do not know which group a given child will fall into— should we really roll the dice? Research demonstrates that the most important predictors of treatment success are the age of the child upon treatment, the duration of symptoms, and whether the children obtains behavioral intervention (Pionek Stone, 2002). We must intervene as soon as possible and effectively to produce the best outcomes in children and adolescents with selective mutism. The time is now.
结论
医生，心理学家，教师和其他人不断赞扬早期干预儿童时期的多种发育和心理障碍。特殊需求的早期干预可以改善结果，减轻病情的影响，并教导他人以成功的方式支持儿童和家庭。选择性缄默症的儿童经常不接受早期干预。最常见的原因包括：
•观察问题（即公开演讲的难度）与诊断/干预之间的时间滞后。如果您正在阅读本书，您可以更好地帮助遏制这种延迟（祝贺）。请考虑通过教育您的学校员工，儿科医生，家人和朋友来帮助其他人了解选择性缄默症。发出信息手册。与他人谈谈你的经历。传播意识。
•难以找到可以提供帮助的专家。这是一个特别棘手的问题，因为许多心理学家对选择性缄默症仍然知之甚少。专门寻找一位有使用行为疗法治疗焦虑症孩子的心理学家，或访问www.selectivemutism.org，www.adaa.org或www.abct.org寻找合适的推荐。
•关注“标记”孩子的问题。相信我，孩子知道他们不会说话。这不是秘密，也不是没有干预就继续下去的正当理由。
•不支持的家庭成员。每个人都有一个家庭成员，他们认为孩子“很好”，或者认为他们所需要的只是在家里更加自律。作为父母最困难的部分之一就是做出不受欢迎的决定，因为它知道从长远来看会改善孩子的生活。
•相信孩子会从中长大。有些SM患儿随着时间和成熟程度的提高而显着改善。并且有些孩子不会随着时间的推移而改善，反而会增加焦虑，减少朋友，并且难以在学校取得成功。我们不知道一个特定的孩子会陷入哪个群体 - 我们真的应该掷骰子吗？研究表明，治疗成功的最重要预测因素是治疗后儿童的年龄，症状的持续时间以及儿童是否获得行为干预（Pionek Stone，2002）。我们必须尽快进行干预，并有效地在选择性缄默症的儿童和青少年中产生最佳结果。就是现在。

STAGES OF SOCIAL OMMUNICATION COMFORT SCALE 

NON-COMMUNICATIVE
—neither non-verbal nor verbal. NO social engagement. 
STAGE 0—NO RESPONDING, NO INITIATING
 – Child stands motionless (stiff body language), expressionless, averts eye gaze, appears “frozen,” MUTE 
OR 
– Seemingly IGNORES person while interacting or speaking to other(s). MUTE towards others For communication to occur, Social Engagement must occur
COMMUNICATIVE  (Nonverbal and/or Verbal)*
*To advance from one stage of communication to the next, increasing social comfort needs to occur. 

STAGE 1—NONVERBAL COMMUNICATION: (NV) 
1A Responding—pointing, nodding, writing, sign language, gesturing, use of “objects” (e.g., whistles, bells, Non-voice augmentative device (e.g., communication boards/cards, symbols, photos) 
1B Initiating—getting someone’s attention via pointing, gesturing, writing, use of “objects” to get attention (e.g., whistles, bells, Non-voice augmentative device (e.g., communication boards/cards, symbols, photos)

STAGE 2—TRANSITION INTO VERBAL COMMUNICATION (TV) 
2A Responding—Via any sounds, (e.g., grunts, animal sounds, letter sounds, moans, etc.): Verbal Intermediary → or Whisper Buddy; Augmentative Device with sound, (e.g., simple message switch, multiple voice message device, tape recorder, video, etc.) 
2B Initiating—Getting someone’s attention via any sounds, (e.g., grunts, animal sounds, letter sounds, moans, etc.): Verbal Intermediary → or Whisper Buddy; Augmentative Device with sound, (e.g., simple message switch, multiple voice message device, tape recorder, video, etc) 

STAGE 3—VERBAL COMMUNICATION (VC) 
3A Responding—Approximate speech/direct speech (e.g., altered or made-up language, baby talk, reading / rehearsing script, soft whispering, speaking) 
3B Initiating—Approximate speech/direct speech (e.g., altered or made-up language, baby talk, reading/rehearsing script, soft whispering, speaking)

社交沟通舒适度的阶段

无沟通 
 - 非言语或口头。没有社交参与。

阶段0-没有响应，没有启始
  - 孩子站立不动（僵硬的肢体语言），无表情，避免目光凝视，出现“冻结”，缄默
或者
 - 在与其他人互动或交谈时看似忽略旁人。对他人缄默。
为了进行沟通，必须进行社交参与

社交沟通（非语言和/或口头）*
*为了从一个阶段的沟通进入下一个阶段，需要增加社交舒适度。

阶段1-非语言沟通：（NV）
1A响应指向，点头，书写，手语，手势，使用“对象”（例如，口哨，铃铛，非语音增强装置（例如，通信板/卡，符号，照片）
1B启动 - 通过指点，手势，写作，使用“对象”引起注意（例如，口哨，铃声，非语音增强设备（例如，通信板/卡，符号，照片））来引起别人的注意

阶段2-转换为口头交流（TV）
2A响应 - 通过任何声音（例如，咕噜声，动物声音，字母声音，呻吟声等）：言语中介→或耳语好友;具有声音的增强设备（例如，简单的消息切换，多个语音消息设备，磁带录音机，视频等）
2B发起 - 通过任何声音引起某人的注意（例如，咕噜声，动物声，字母声，呻吟声等）：言语中介→或悄悄话;具有声音的增强设备（例如，简单的消息切换，多个语音消息设备，录音机，视频等）

阶段3-语言交流（VC）
3A回应 - 近似语音/直接言语（例如，改变或化妆语言，婴儿谈话，阅读/排练脚本，软耳语，口语）
3B发起/起始 - 近似语音/直接语音（例如，改变或化妆语言，婴儿谈话，阅读/排练脚本，软耳语，说话）

STIMULUS FADING PROCEDURE EXAMPLE
 
1. Goal is to get new communication partner (NCP) into room with child using appropriate speech. Parent or keyworker’s job is to keep child talking while the NCP enters through fun activities that encourage speech, forced-choice questions, and at least five-second latency to response. 
2. Parent/keyworker and child should have some time to “warm up” in the room, playing and talking alone with the door almost closed. During this time, full speech (not a quiet whisper) must be obtained consistently before a new individual begins fading in. (***If several sessions pass and the child cannot warm up to the point of consistent speech, intervention must shift to shaping). 
3. NCP begins fading into the room when the parent/keyworker has obtained an appropriate volume of consistent speech from the child. The NCP begins by simply opening the door and walking in front of the door, so as to make her presence known. The NCP should NOT respond in any way to what the child says or act as if they are attending to the child. 
4. The NCP should very slowly enter the room, as far away from the child as possible, and busy themselves (i.e., type on the computer, pick up the room, or do paperwork). If the child begins to discontinue speaking or shows other general signs of anxiety, slow the entrance down even more or just stay in the same position for several minutes until the anxiety is reduced and speech resumes. 
5. The NCP should slowly start making their way toward the child by finding things to do in closer proximity to the interaction, but should continue to pay no attention to the child. 
6. When the NCP is close to the interaction and the child has maintained speech, he or she can begin to silently attend to the interaction. 
7. If the child tolerates attention to their speech, the NCP can begin to reflect or respond to what the child is saying. For example, if the child tells the parent that they picked a blue card, the NCP could reflect, “Oh, you picked a blue card.” If the child tells the mother that he played his new videogame last night, the NCP could comment, “You played your new game—that sounds like fun.” 
8. When this seems comfortable and minimally anxiety-provoking, the NCP can begin peppering in occasional forced-choice questions. 
9. When the child is consistently responding to the NCP, the parent can begin to slowly fade out of the room, leaving the NCP and the child. This can be done using the above steps in reverse. It is important that the parent not fade faster than the child can manage. If significant anxiety is observed or if the child’s verbalizations considerably decrease, the parent should remain at the current location until comfort is re-established.
If the child ceases speech at any point during the stimulus fading, the NCP should stop moving toward the child/parent and wait for the parent to re-establish speech with the child. In the case that the parent is unable to re-establish speech in a few minutes, the NCP should move back a few steps toward the door, and again see if the parent is able to re-establish speech with the child. If the parents is still unable to re-establish speech, shaping may need to be considered in the place of stimulus fading as the initial intervention.
If the NCP is unable to complete all steps in one session, they could start outside of the room again but attempt to fade in more quickly. As the child’s anxiety will likely spontaneously return somewhat at the beginning of each session (although less and less will be present each time), the NCP should still be careful to be responsive to the anxiety.

刺激渐变程序示例
 
1.目标是让新的沟通伙伴（NCP）与孩子一起使用适当的演讲。家长或关键工作者的工作是让孩子在NCP通过鼓励言语，强制选择问题和至少5秒的响应延迟的有趣活动进入时进行交谈。
2.家长/关键工作者和孩子应该有一些时间在房间里“热身”，单独玩耍和谈话，门几乎关闭。在这段时间内，必须在新人开始消退之前始终获得完整的演讲（不是安静的低语）。（***如果几个课程通过，孩子不能热身到一致的讲话，干预必须转向塑造）。
3.当父母/关键工作者从孩子那里获得适当数量的一致语音时，NCP开始淡入房间。 NCP开始只需打开门并走在门前，以便让她知晓。 NCP不应以任何方式回应孩子说的话或表现好像他们正在照顾孩子一样。
4. NCP应该非常缓慢地进入房间，尽可能远离孩子，并且自己忙碌（即，在电脑上打字，拿起房间或做文书工作）。如果孩子开始停止说话或表现出其他一般的焦虑迹象，请将入口减慢甚至更多，或者只是停留在相同位置几分钟，直到焦虑减少并恢复讲话。
5. NCP应该通过在更接近互动时找到要做的事情慢慢开始向孩子开始，但是应该继续不关注孩子。
6.当NCP接近互动并且孩子保持言语时，他或她可以开始默默地参与互动。
7.如果孩子容忍注意他们的讲话，NCP可以开始反映或回应孩子说的话。例如，如果孩子告诉父母他们选择了蓝卡，NCP可以反映：“哦，你选了一张蓝卡。”如果孩子告诉母亲他昨晚播放了他的新视频游戏，NCP可以发表评论，“你玩过你的新游戏 - 听起来很有趣。”
8.当这看起来很舒服并且极其焦虑时，NCP可以在偶尔的强制选择问题中开始发挥作用。
9.当孩子一直回应NCP时，父母可以开始慢慢淡出房间，离开NCP和孩子。这可以使用上述步骤反过来完成。重要的是，父母不要比孩子能够管理的更快地消退。如果观察到明显的焦虑或者孩子的言语明显减少，父母应该留在当前位置，直到重新建立安慰。

如果孩子在刺激消退期间的任何时候停止发言，NCP应该停止向孩子/父母移动并等待父母与孩子重新建立讲话。如果父母在几分钟内无法重新建立语音，NCP应向后退几步，并再次查看父母是否能够与孩子重新建立语音。如果父母仍然无法重新建立言语，则可能需要考虑在刺激消退的地方进行整形作为初始干预。
如果NCP无法在一个会话中完成所有步骤，他们可以再次从房间外面开始，但尝试更快地淡入。由于孩子的焦虑可能会在每次开始时自发地有所回复（尽管每次都会越来越少），NCP仍然应该小心对焦虑做出反应。

UNDERSTANDING SELECTIVE MUTISM HANDOUT
Selective mutism is a childhood anxiety disorder characterized by a child’s ‘inability’ to speak in various social settings. These children are able to speak freely at home, and in settings where they are comfortable, secure and relaxed. Selective mutism is due to severe anxiety, not oppositional behavior directed towards school personnel—the lack of communication serves to reduce anxiety and protect the child from further anxiety-provoking social interactions. It is so important for teachers, school personnel, and other adults to remember that the child with selective mutism is not ‘being silent’ or barely whispering ‘on purpose,’ or trying to ‘control’ a situation. These children literally cannot speak.
The majority of selectively mute children have inhibited temperaments. When children who are selectively mute confront a social situation where they do not feel comfortable, they often become mute and possibly non-communicative. Some children may have great difficulty pointing/nodding or even mouthing words while others may be able to respond and initiate nonverbally with ease and ‘look’ completely relaxed.
理解选择性缄默症讲义
选择性缄默症是一种儿童焦虑症，其特征是儿童在各种社交环境中“不能”说话。这些孩子可以在家里自由发言，在舒适，安全和放松的环境中畅所欲言。选择性缄默症是由于严重的焦虑，而不是针对学校人员的反对行为 - 缺乏沟通有助于减少焦虑并保护儿童免受进一步引起焦虑的社交互动。对于教师，学校工作人员和其他成年人来说，记住选择性缄默症的孩子并非“保持沉默”或者“故意”或者试图“控制”某种情况，这一点非常重要。这些孩子实际上不会说话。
大多数选择性静音的孩子都抑制了性情。当有选择性地静音的孩子面对他们感到不舒服的社交场合时，他们经常变得沉默并且可能是非交际的。有些孩子可能很难指点/点头甚至嘴里说话，而其他孩子则可能能够轻松地响应和启动非语言并且“看起来”完全放松。

School is usually the most difficult place for children with selective mutism to be. What can an adult do to help the anxious child with selective mutism?
• Primarily, the adult should try to get to know the child in a completely unobtrusive and accepting manner. Visiting the child at home is often beneficial. There is certainly no better place for children to feel more comfortable then in their own home! Visiting the children on their own turf will certainly allow for a more comfortable way of getting to know each other.
• Be patient with the child. Although he/she may seem stubborn, it is believed that the silence or non-communicative behavior simply helps him avoid extremely high levels of discomfort and anxiety. The child is likely to be scared rather than angry or stubborn.
• Sometimes present questions in a multiple-choice format (e.g., “Would you rather play with Legos or Playdoh?”). If he/she does not answer, wait 5 seconds and ask the question again.
• Please be careful about describing the child as shy—this may seem like a kind response but it leads the child and others to believe that the behavior cannot change.
• Quietly praise the child and provide support when he uses forms of communication that involve reaching out and responding to others. Nonchalantly giving praise like “Thanks for letting me know” or “I’m glad you told me” is appropriate.
• Actively support the child’s inclusion and ensure peers do not interpret silence as
disinterest/rudeness.
Try to avoid:
• Guessing at what the child is telling you nonverbally. Spending a long time in nonverbal interactions may diminish the child’s motivation for using words at a later time.
• Pressuring, bribing, threatening, flattering, or cajoling the child into speaking.
• Completely removing opportunities for children speak—if children do not have any opportunity or reason to speak, they probably will not speak.
对于有选择性缄默症的孩子来说，学校通常是最困难的地方。成年人可以做些什么来帮助焦虑的孩子选择性缄默？
•成年人应该尽量以完全不引人注意和接受的方式了解孩子。在家里探望孩子通常是有益的。对于孩子来说，在自己的家中感觉更舒适当然没有比这更好的地方了！在他们自己的草坪上拜访孩子肯定会让他们更加舒适地相互了解。
•对孩子保持耐心。虽然他/她可能看起来很顽固，但人们认为沉默或非交际行为只会帮助他避免极度的不适和焦虑。孩子可能会害怕而不是生气或顽固。
•有时以多种选择格式呈现问题（例如，“您更愿意与乐高积木或Playdoh一起玩吗？”）。如果他/她没有回答，请等待5秒钟再次提问。
•请小心将孩子描述为害羞 - 这似乎是一种善意的反应，但它会引导孩子和其他人相信行为不会改变。
•在使用涉及接触和回应他人的沟通方式时，悄悄地赞扬孩子并提供支持。毫无疑问地给予赞美，比如“谢谢你让我知道”或“我很高兴你告诉我”是合适的。
•积极支持孩子的参与，并确保同伴不要将沉默解释为不感兴趣/粗鲁。
尽量避免：
•猜测孩子非语言地告诉你什么。花费很长时间进行非语言互动可能会降低孩子以后使用语言交流的动机。
•对孩子施压，贿赂，威胁，奉承或哄骗说话。
•彻底消除孩子说话的机会 - 如果孩子没有任何机会或理由说话，他们可能不会说话。

 SAMPLE SCHOOL LETTER
Letter Requesting Consideration of an Individualized Education Plan Everything between brackets should be customized. Be sure to edit content in order to reflect your child’s specific situation. Be sure to send a copy to the principal and/or school psychologist or counselor and keep a copy for yourself.
[Date] 
[School] 
[Address] 
[City, State, Zip Code]
To Whom it May Concern,
My child, [child’s name], is currently in [list program or grade] at [school name]. Given a recent diagnostic evaluation with [Dr’s Name] and a subsequent diagnosis of selective mutism, a specific anxiety disorder, I would like to meet with the special education team to discuss [his/her] current placement situation and to look at how we can modify [his/her] current goals and objectives to better fit [his/her] educational needs.
(Edit this paragraph to reflect your specific situation: My child is regressing. [He/she] is not benefiting from [his/her] current placement. I am concerned that [he/she] is unable to participate in oral presentations and group projects. If [he/she] is injured, [he/she] may not be able to indicate that [he/she] needs help.)
Children with Selective mutism need specific intervention plans in the school setting, including working with a “keyworker”—someone in the school setting who can practice “being brave” and encouraging speech with (child’s name). [Dr’s name] is happy to assist in setting up an intervention plan with this keyworker, as well as helping to develop accommodations in the classroom.
Please schedule a meeting as soon as possible so that we can discuss these issues and consider an accommodations and/or intervention plan. I would prefer being contacted with time options in advance so that the meeting can be scheduled at a mutually agreeable time and place.
I look forward to hearing from you within the next five school days.
Sincerely,
[Parent/Guardian Name] [Address] [City, State, Zip Code] [Phone Number]

选择性缄默症的儿童需要在学校环境中采取具体的干预计划，包括与“关键工作者”一起工作 - 在学校环境中的某些人可以练习“勇敢”并鼓励用（孩子的名字）讲话。 [博士的名字]很乐意协助与这位关键工作者制定干预计划，并帮助在课堂上开发住宿。

我期待在接下来的五个上学日内收到你的来信。

SAMPLE SELECTIVE MUTISM INTERVENTION AND ACCOMMODATION PLAN RECOMMENDATIONS
(*Not all listed interventions and accommodations are appropriate for every child, thus, interventions should be chosen based on the needs of the child and developed in conjunction with the intervention team with the goal of meeting those needs.)
示例 选择性缄默症的干预和帮助计划建议
（*并非所有列出的干预措施和帮助都适合每个儿童，因此，应根据儿童的需求选择干预措施，并与干预小组一起制定干预措施，以满足这些需求。）

1. [Student] may benefit from working in individualized sessions with a “keyworker” (a school- based professional in charge of practicing “brave work” with the child, generalizing to all school environments, and communicating with other involved team members). These sessions should consist of behavioral interventions, using shaping and stimulus fading procedures to reduce the child’s anxiety and increase communication at a slow, steady pace. More information about the development of an intervention plan within the school can be found in Selective Mutism by Aimee Kotrba, Ph.D. This intervention must begin one-on-one in a closed-office setting, and once speech has been obtained the child and keyworker can begin working in different settings and including an increasing number of individuals. 
1. [学生]可以通过与“关键工作者”（一名负责与孩子一起实施“勇敢工作”的学校专业人员，向所有学校环境推广，以及与其他相关团队成员沟通）的个性化会议中受益。这些会议应该包括行为干预，使用塑形和刺激衰退程序来减少孩子的焦虑，并以缓慢，稳定的速度增加沟通。关于在学校内制定干预计划的更多信息可以在Aimee Kotrba博士的选择性突变中找到。这种干预必须在封闭的办公环境中一对一地开始，一旦获得言论，孩子和关键工作者就可以开始在不同的环境中工作，包括越来越多的个人。

2. [Student] should at first be encouraged to increase communication and participate in the classroom and socially, even if that communication is nonverbal at first. Eventually, attempts should be made to reinforce and attend to only verbal language, so as to extinguish the use of nonverbal communication. 
2. [学生]首先应该被鼓励增加沟通，参与课堂和社交活动，即使这种沟通最初是非语言的。最终，应该尝试加强和只关注口头语言，以便消除非语言交流的使用。

3. Allow [student] extra time to respond to questions. At least five seconds should be provided after every communication prompt or question, in order to all [student] an opportunity to answer. 
3.让[学生]有更多时间回答问题。每次通讯提示或问题后至少应提供五秒钟，以便所有[学生]有机会回答。

4. Take care not to make too much direct eye contact, as this can increase [student’s] anxiety. 
4.注意不要过多地直接接触眼睛，因为这会增加[学生]的焦虑。

5. [Student] should be given alternative ways of completing oral assignments if necessary, including written projects, audio/videotaping the child presenting at home, allowing a peer or the teacher to present the written work while the child points to a poster board, etc. 
5.如有必要，[学生]应有其他方式完成口头作业，包括书面项目，在家中录制儿童的录音/录像，允许同伴或教师在孩子指向海报板时提交书面作业，等等

6. The classroom teacher will provide nonverbal assessments. 
6.课堂教师将提供非语言评估。

7. [Student] should never be punished and/or criticized, and should not receive reduced grades because of the lack of speech. 
7. [学生]不应该受到惩罚和/或批评，并且由于缺乏言论，不应该得到降低的分数。

8. When the child does communicate, no extra attention or overt praise should be given to the child, as this might increase anxiety. Matter-of-fact praise (such as “Thanks for letting me know”) can be given nonchalantly. 
8.当孩子进行交流时，不应给予孩子额外的关注或公开表扬，因为这可能会增加焦虑。事实上的称赞（例如“感谢让我知道”）可以毫无顾忌地给予。

9. [Student] shall be permitted, if necessary, to take any standardized test outside of the regular classroom, either privately or in a small group, administered by the keyworker, in case [she/he] needs to ask a question or seek clarification. 
9.如果[她/他]需要提出问题或寻求，必要时，[学生]可以在普通教室外进行任何标准化测试，无论是私人还是小组，由主要工作人员管理。澄清。

10. Home/school communication will be available on a daily or weekly basis with both the classroom teacher and the “key worker.” It is recommended that this communication take the form of a “Brave Work Log” (as seen in ______________ by Aimee Kotrba, Ph.D.). Copies can be placed in a 3-ring binder and passed around to team members. All team members (e.g., teacher, keyworker, psychologist, parent) can record progress so as to maximize communication. 
10.家庭/学校的沟通将每天或每周与课堂教师和“关键工作者”一起提供。建议此沟通采取“勇敢的工作日志”的形式（如______________ Aimee所见） Kotrba，博士。副本可以放在一个3环活页夹中，并传递给团队成员。所有团队成员（例如，教师，键盘工作者，心理学家，家长）都可以记录进度以最大化沟通。

11. Assign [Student] a seat with a close friend on each side, close to the teacher, away from door. 
11.为[学生]分配一个座位，每侧都有一位亲密的朋友，靠近老师，远离门。

12. Assign [Student Name] a seat at lunch next to friends. 
12.分配[学生姓名]午餐时坐在朋友的旁边。

13. Assign scheduled times for bathroom breaks. Have [Student] and [her/his] buddy accompany each other to the bathroom. 
13.预定时间去厕所的。让[学生]和[她/他]的伙伴互相陪伴到厕所。

14. Prepare [Student] for changes in routine (e.g., substitute teachers) and special large group activities (e.g., substitutes, fire drills, schedule changes, field trips). All substitute teachers should be made aware of the child’s accommodations.
14.准备[学生]对例行日常出现的变更（例如代课教师）和特殊大型团体活动（例如替补，消防演习，时间表变更，实地考察）。所有代课教师都应该了解孩子的帮助计划。

15. All adults who come into contact with [Student] should be advised of the intervention plan (e.g., teacher, extras teachers, lunch personnel, office staff, etc.). 
15.应告知所有与[学生]接触的成年人（例如，教师，临时教师，午餐人员，办公室工作人员等）。
16. The classroom teacher may provide individual rapport-building time with [Student] and possibly a friend or two, before/after school or during lunch. This will allow [Student] extra time to build comfort, and thereby increase the likelihood of communication. 
16.课堂教师可以在学生之前/之后或午餐时间与[学生]和可能的一两个朋友提供个人关系建立时间。这将允许[学生]额外的时间来建立舒适度，从而增加沟通的可能性。

17. Allow [Student] to spend time with [her/his] teacher before the school year starts. This may take the form of working with the child and keyworker to transfer speech prior to the end of the previous school year, and/or may include coming into the school in the days prior to school beginning to get comfortable and develop rapport with the new teacher. The open- house is not adequate, since it is generally short, conducted in a group, and may be chaotic and anxiety-provoking for [Student]. Instead, these meetings should be only the teacher, child, and parent or keyworker, and should be done in a relaxed and fun manner. 
17.允许[学生]在学年开始前与[他/她]老师共度时光。这可能采取与孩子和关键工作者合作的形式，在上一学年结束之前转移语音，和/或可能包括在学校开始之前的几天进入学校，以便与新学生建立融洽关系。老师。开放日是不够的，因为它通常很短，在一个小组中进行，并且对于[学生]可能是混乱和焦虑的。相反，这些会议应该只是教师，孩子，父母或关键工作者，应该以轻松和有趣的方式完成。

18. Allow [Student]’s parents/family members to have access to the school environment during off hours (before and after school, during summer months) to promote comfort and eventually verbalization when alone with a parent and eventually, one or more peers or a teacher. 
18.允许[学生]的父母/家庭成员在非工作时间（学前和课后，夏季）进入学校环境，以便在与父母一起并最终与一个或多个同伴一起时提高舒适度并最终进行语言表达还是老师

19. If the child is working with a clinical psychologist outside of the school setting, consider inviting the psychologist into the school for sessions in order to generalize “brave work” into the school setting. Minimally, the psychologist should be invited to participate in team meetings in the school. 
19.如果孩子在学校以外与临床心理学家合作，请考虑邀请心理学家进入学校参加课程，以便将“勇敢的工作”推广到学校环境中。最低限度，应邀请心理学家参加学校的团队会议。

20. Each year [Student] should be moved up to the next teacher with his/her close friends, particularly with those to whom he/she speaks. [Student] will be placed with accepting teachers to be chosen with team and parent input. 
20.每年[学生]应该与他/她的亲密朋友，特别是与他/她说话的人交往下一位老师。 [学生]将与接受教师一起选择与团队和家长的投入。

21. The district will provide faculty training through consultations and attendance at SM conferences. Appropriate workshops and seminars can be found at www.selectivemutism.org on the calendar of events. 
21.学区将通过协商和参加SM会议提供教师培训。可在www.selectivemutism.org上的活动日程表上找到适当的研讨会和研讨会。

22. The district will purchase selective mutism educational materials to educate the child’s teachers and all school personnel. A training video for school professionals on treating selective mutism is available at www.selectivemutismtreatment.com.
22.学区将购买选择性缄默教育材料，以教育儿童的教师和所有学校人员。有关治疗选择性缄默症的学校专业人士的培训视频，请访问www.selectivemutismtreatment.com。
23. The school will provide quarterly parent/faculty meetings to update and report upon accommodations and progress.
23.学校将提供季度家长/教师会议，以更新和报告住宿和进展情况。
 
24. Resources on selective mutism should be utilized for further suggestions and information about [Student]’s condition. Please visit the Selective Mutism Group at www.selectivemutism.org for up-to-date information for teachers, professionals and parents. It is recommended that all individuals within the school who will be acting as interventionists (e.g., keyworkers, teachers, etc.) read Selective Mutism by Aimee Kotrba, Ph.D., in order to fully understand the conceptualization of Selective mutism as well as the details and workings of the intervention plan.
24.应利用有关选择性缄默的资源，提供有关[学生]病情的进一步建议和信息。请访问www.selectivemutism.org上的选择性Mutism小组，获取教师，专业人士和家长的最新信息。建议学校内将扮演干预主义者的所有个人（如关键工作者，教师等）阅读Aimee Kotrba博士的选择性突变，以充分理解选择性缄默症的概念化以及干预计划的细节和运作。

AUTHOR BIOGRAPHY
Aimee Kotrba, Ph.D, is a licensed clinical psychologist specializing in the expert assessment and treatment of Selective Mutism and Social Anxiety. Dr. Kotrba is the owner and lead psychologist at Thriving Minds Behavioral Clinic in Brighton, Michigan, a specialty clinic dedicated to effective treatment for children with Selective Mutism and other pediatric anxiety disorders.
Dr. Kotrba currently serves as the president of the Selective Mutism Group (SMG) Board of Directors and as the psychological expert for a local Selective Mutism parent support group. Her work has appeared in numerous media outlets, including Livingston County Parent Journal and MetroParent Magazine. She is a nationally recognized speaker and regularly offers local, national, and international workshops on the identification and treatment of Selective Mutism for parents, professionals, and school personnel.

作者简历
Aimee Kotrba博士是一名持牌临床心理学家，专门从事选择性突变和社交焦虑的专家评估和治疗。 Kotrba博士是密歇根州布莱顿Thriving Minds Behavioral Clinic的所有者和首席心理学家，这是一家致力于为选择性突变和其他儿童焦虑症患儿提供有效治疗的专科诊所。
Kotrba博士目前担任选择性Muttivity小组（SMG）董事会主席和当地选择性突变主义家长支持小组的心理专家。她的作品曾出现在众多媒体上，包括利文斯顿县家长杂志和MetroParent杂志。她是全国公认的演讲者，并定期为家长，专业人士和学校人员提供有关选择性突变的识别和治疗的地方，国家和国际研讨会。
